A missing training_outcome.json means either a baseline-only run or a run that crashed in training (Module 6: crash.json is written, training_outcome.json isn't). The message asserts the first. A one-line check for crash.json would give the right diagnosis. analyze-failures has the same pattern: "this run had no test-set model evaluation" could also mean "crashed before evaluation".

compare usage is documented two ways. scripts/compare.py's docstring says python scripts/compare.py --runs a b c. With a Typer list[Path] option, that form fails: after the first value, b and c are unexpected extra arguments. The README (line 186, "--runs repeated once per run") and docs/API_REFERENCE.md §4.7 show the correct repeated-flag form. The script wrapper's docstring is wrong.

12.3 train

@app.command()
def train(config: Path = typer.Option(..., "--config"), seed: Optional[int] = typer.Option(None, "--seed")):
    """Run one experiment end to end: intervention -> train -> evaluate -> report."""
    cfg = load_experiment_config(config)
    result = ExperimentRunner().run(cfg, seed=seed)
    console.print(f"run complete: {result.run_dir}")
    _print_json(result.summary)
  • It's a thin wrapper over Modules 2 and 11, which is correct: one implementation.
  • --seed is the Module 11 bug at its entry point. The help text gives no hint that it only names the directory.
  • No --base (Module 2 gap 9), no seed loop over config.seeds, and no --force for a failed same-day run (Module 3 gap 3).
  • Exceptions surface as raw tracebacks. For a research CLI, that's defensible: the traceback is the diagnosis. But a LookupError("unknown version: so100_pickplace@clean/v9") would be more readable as a one-line error with exit code 2.

12.4 evaluate and test: the divergent re-implementation

@app.command()
def evaluate(run: Path = typer.Option(..., "--run"), split: str = typer.Option("test", "--split")):
    """Re-run stratified + baseline offline evaluation for an existing run's
    selected checkpoint against SPLIT, overwriting its evaluation/ artifacts."""
    from rde.catalog import Catalog
    ...
    from model_training.evaluation import episode_metrics, error_distribution, evaluate_baselines, stratified_evaluate, temporal_error

    dataset_json = json.loads((run / "dataset.json").read_text())
    model_json = json.loads((run / "model.json").read_text())
    checkpoint = model_json.get("selected_checkpoint")
    if not checkpoint:
        console.print(f"[yellow]no selected_checkpoint recorded for run {run}[/yellow]")
        raise typer.Exit(1)

    settings = get_settings()
    view = DataEngine(settings).get_dataset(dataset_json["dataset_id"], dataset_json["training_version"])
    try:
        policy = SmolVLAPolicy.from_checkpoint(checkpoint)
        adapter = VisionLanguageAdapter(view.contract)
        strat = stratified_evaluate(view, policy, adapter, base_split=split, slices=["per_task"])
        overall = strat.pop("overall")
        payload = {"summary": overall.summary(), "metrics": overall.metrics,
                   "error_distribution": error_distribution(overall),
                   "episode_metrics": episode_metrics(overall),
                   "temporal_error": temporal_error(overall, view)}
        eval_dir = run / "evaluation"
        eval_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
        (eval_dir / "test_overall.json").write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2, default=str))
        (eval_dir / "stratified.json").write_text(json.dumps({...}, indent=2, default=str))
        _print_json(payload["summary"])
    finally:
        view.close()

What's right: it reopens the exact training_version recorded in dataset.json (data by identity, never by path), reloads the selected checkpoint, and closes the view in finally. Re-scoring the same version on the same split scores the same episodes.

What diverges from the runner's stage 4 (Module 11):

AspectRunnermtrain evaluate
Baselinesre-fit and scorednot run. evaluate_baselines is imported and never called, despite the docstring's "Re-run stratified + baseline"
Slicesconfig.evaluation.slices ([per_task, per_length_bucket] from base.yaml)hard-coded ["per_task"]
batch_size / max_framesfrom config (16 / null)stratified_evaluate defaults (32 / None)
Predictions filepredictions/test_outcomes.jsonl writtennot rewritten, so it now describes the old predictions
metrics/test.jsonl, summary.json, model_artifact.json, report, model card, feedback, indexwrittennone updated

Consequences:

  1. It overwrites the reported numbers in place. SmolVLA is stochastic (Module 7: up to 5% between re-scorings), so running mtrain test on a published run changes test_overall.json. The README's numbers can no longer be traced to the file they came from, and there's no backup or history. The rest of the system treats a run directory as write-once (make_run_dir refuses to overwrite). This command quietly breaks that rule for the most important file in it.
  2. It destroys the slices. The original stratified.json (task plus three length slices) is replaced by per-task only. That's information loss, not just a re-score.
  3. It leaves the directory internally inconsistent. test_overall.json disagrees with summary.json, metrics/test.jsonl, model_artifact.json["metrics"], the report, the model card, predictions/test_outcomes.jsonl and the feedback artifact computed from the old outcomes.
  4. --split val writes validation results into test_overall.json. docs/API_REFERENCE.md §4.5 and §9.3 recommend mtrain evaluate --run <dir> --split val. The doc honestly says it overwrites test_overall.json, but the filename then lies, and mtrain compare, plot_results.py and load_run_summary will all read validation metrics as test metrics. On a project whose central discipline is val/test separation, this is the most dangerous documented workflow in the repo.
  5. Unused imports (Catalog, evaluate_baselines) are leftovers from the fuller version the docstring still describes.

What it should be: a call into the same evaluation function the runner uses, factored out of ExperimentRunner.run (a run_evaluation_stage(run_dir, config, view, checkpoint) that takes its settings from the run's own config.resolved.yaml), writing to a new, versioned directory (evaluation/rescore_<timestamp>_<split>/) and never over the original. That gives one implementation, no overwrite, and filenames named for the split.

@app.command()
def test(run: Path = typer.Option(..., "--run")):
    """Alias for `evaluate --split test` -- the offline test-set score."""
    evaluate(run=run, split="test")

Calling a Typer command function directly works, because it's still a plain Python function. Note that the alias inherits everything above. "mtrain test" sounds like a harmless check, but it overwrites the published test numbers.

12.5 rollout: honesty as a command

@app.command()
def rollout(run: Path = typer.Option(..., "--run")):
    """Closed-loop rollout evaluation. No simulator or robot is connected in
    this environment, so this reports 'unavailable' rather than fabricating a
    success rate -- never conflated with offline evaluation."""
    payload = {"status": "unavailable", "reason": "no simulator or physical robot connected in this environment"}
    (run / "metrics").mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    with (run / "metrics" / "rollout.jsonl").open("a") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(payload) + "\n")
    _print_json(payload)

Decision: a command whose only job is to record that something can't be measured. Why: docs/API_REFERENCE.md §4.6 says "absence is a recorded, honest fact rather than a silently missing file". It also gives the interface a slot where a real rollout harness would plug in. Cost: it appends a duplicate record each time it's run (the runner already wrote one), with no timestamp to distinguish them. mkdir(exist_ok=True) without parents=True raises on a mistyped run path whose parent doesn't exist. That's arguably the right failure, though it's a raw traceback.

12.6 export: "paper-ready" with gaps

output.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
for sub in ("figures", "tables", "metrics", "configs", "manifests"):
    (output / sub).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
# figures ← reports/figures/*; metrics ← evaluation/*.json; configs ← config.resolved.yaml;
# manifests ← manifest.json, dataset.json, model.json, dataset_profile.json (if present)
(output / "experiment_summary.md").write_text(
    "... Every file under figures/, tables/, metrics/, configs/, manifests/ traces back to the "
    f"run directory above. Regenerate with `mtrain export --run {run} --output {output}`.\n")
  • tables/ is created and never filled. No code copies anything into it.
  • manifest.json never exists (Module 3, S12).
  • git.json and environment.json aren't exported. The bundle that claims everything "traces back" omits the commits and environment, which are exactly what a reader of a paper artifact needs to reproduce it. (It does keep the hostname out, as Module 3 noted, but it drops the commits along with it.)
  • reports/experiment_report.md, reports/model_card.md and predictions/test_outcomes.jsonl aren't exported, so neither the narrative nor the per-frame evidence is included.
  • It overwrites an existing export silently (exist_ok=True plus copy2). That's the opposite policy to make_run_dir. For a derived bundle that can be regenerated, it's defensible, but stale files from a previous export into the same directory remain.
  • "Nothing is recomputed or summarized lossily" (API_REFERENCE §4.9) is true for what is copied, but the selection of what to copy is lossy in the most important places.

12.7 build-intervention: resolving the design rule

@app.command("build-intervention")
def build_intervention_cmd(dataset, version, kind, variable=None, variable_value=None,
                           oversample_factor: float = 3.0, target_config: Path = ...,
                           reason: str = "", dry_run: bool = typer.Option(True, "--dry-run/--apply")):
    """... Deliberately does not build the dataset version itself -- `mtrain train`
    does that as part of a normal run, with real lineage. This command only
    resolves *which* episodes/weights the intervention names, from the
    dataset's actual per-episode metadata, and never presumes them."""

What's right:

  • Dry run by default. It prints the resolved block, and you must pass --apply to write. For a command that rewrites a config file, that's exactly the right default.
  • Resolution and materialization are separate. It names episodes; train builds the version, so lineage is created once, by the normal path. This is the command that operationalizes "the weak dimension must not be presumed" (Module 2): it reads the real catalog and resolves from real metadata.
  • task_id values are converted to int (value = int(variable_value)). That avoids Module 2's string-vs-int silent no-op on the generated path.
  • An empty result exits with an error for rebalanced ("resolved zero episodes").

What's wrong:

  • --apply destroys the config's comments. yaml.safe_load followed by yaml.safe_dump(data, sort_keys=False) rewrites the whole file. The shipped imbalanced.yaml and rebalanced.yaml carry 15–20-line header comments explaining why they're templates and what the result must and mustn't claim ("do not claim a fixed weak regime unless the intervention and the evidence both support it"). Applying the resolved intervention deletes that reasoning and reflows the > folded hypothesis strings. The safeguards written for the human are erased at the moment they matter. ruamel.yaml round-trip mode, or writing the block to a separate *.intervention.yaml that the loader merges, fixes this.
  • Imbalanced is written as kind: custom. block = {"kind": "custom", "episode_ids": ..., "variable": ..., "variable_value": ..., "reason": ...}. The run's dataset.json will then say kind: custom, and summary.intervention, the index, and mtrain compare's "intervention" column all lose the fact that this was the imbalanced experiment. Only tags.intervention: imbalanced_data preserves it, and only because the template sets that tag.
  • Imbalanced inherits the silent no-op (Module 2 gap 4). --kind imbalanced --variable episode_length_bucket with no --variable-value passes None, resolve_episode_subset keeps every episode, and the command prints and (with --apply) writes a custom block listing all episode IDs. The rebalanced path checks for an empty result; the imbalanced path doesn't check for a full result.
  • _target_episodes is copied from interventions.py. The CLI defines its own _target_episodes(var, val) with the same body as the closure inside build_intervention_dataset. That's two implementations of "which episodes have attribute = value", which will drift. It should be one public function in data/interventions.py.
  • A missing target config produces a file with only intervention:. data = yaml.safe_load(...) if target_config.exists() else {}, then writing it gives a config with no experiment_name or dataset. That fails later at load, which is acceptable, but it's an odd thing to allow.
  • It resolves against --version, which may differ from the config's dataset.version. Nothing checks that the version the episodes were resolved against matches the version the config will train from. If they differ, the episode IDs may not exist in the training input.

12.8 The script wrappers

#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Thin wrapper: `python scripts/train.py --config configs/experiments/baseline.yaml`.
Identical to `mtrain train`; provided for environments where the console script is not on the path."""
import sys
from model_training.cli import app

if __name__ == "__main__":
    app(["train", *sys.argv[1:]])
  • Seven 8–9-line files, one per command, each prepending the sub-command name. They're all identical in shape. Why: environments where mtrain isn't on PATH. Cost: they still import model_training, so the package must be installed. They help only when the virtualenv's bin/ isn't on PATH, and python -m model_training.cli train ... already covers that, because cli.py has if __name__ == "__main__": main(). So they're redundant convenience. That's harmless, but it's seven more files whose docstrings can drift, and compare.py's already has.
  • There are no wrappers for inspect, profile, validate or build-intervention, so the set is partial.
  • scripts/test.py's name. Running python scripts/test.py puts scripts/ first on sys.path, which shadows the standard library's test package for that process. It's harmless here (nothing imports test), but it's the sort of name that bites later.

12.9 Honest gaps to own before an interviewer finds them

  1. mtrain evaluate/test overwrites test_overall.json and stratified.json in place, for a stochastic model, with no history. Consequence: published numbers can't be traced to their file after a re-score, and a write-once run directory becomes mutable.
  2. --split val writes validation metrics into test_overall.json, and the docs recommend it. Consequence: compare, plot_results.py and load_run_summary will read val as test.
  3. evaluate diverges from the runner: no baselines (despite its docstring), hard-coded per_task slices, default batch settings, no update to predictions, summary, metrics, report, card, artifact or feedback. Consequence: a re-scored run directory is internally inconsistent.
  4. build-intervention --apply erases the template's explanatory comments and reflows text. Consequence: the written-down safeguards disappear at the moment of use.
  5. Imbalanced is written as kind: custom. Consequence: the intervention label is lost from dataset.json, the summary, the index and the comparison.
  6. build-intervention --kind imbalanced without --variable-value silently selects every episode. Consequence: a "resolved" imbalance that's a full run.
  7. _target_episodes is duplicated between the CLI and interventions.py. Consequence: drift risk in the core selection rule.
  8. The resolution version isn't checked against the config's training input version. Consequence: episode IDs may not exist where training happens.
  9. export omits git.json, environment.json, reports and predictions; tables/ is always empty; manifest.json never exists. Consequence: the "paper-ready" bundle lacks the provenance it claims.
  10. validate and analyze-failures misdiagnose crashed runs as "no training" or "no evaluation". Consequence: misleading triage.
  11. scripts/compare.py's docstring shows --runs a b c, which Typer rejects. Consequence: a wrong usage example.
  12. --seed help doesn't say it only names the directory; there's no --base, --force, seed loop, report or sweep command. Consequence: the CLI can't express multi-seed studies, report regeneration or sweeps.
  13. inspect --version defaults to training/v1. Consequence: an arbitrary data default that may not exist.
  14. Script wrappers are redundant with python -m model_training.cli, incomplete, and one shadows stdlib test. Consequence: maintenance surface for no capability.
  15. No CLI tests (grep finds none). Consequence: every CLI-only behaviour above is unexercised.

The shape to carry into Module 13

 mtrain (Typer; only typer/yaml/rich at import — rde loaded per command)
 ├─ STATE-CHANGING
 │   train --config [--seed≠seed]        → load_experiment_config → ExperimentRunner.run   (one impl ✓)
 │   evaluate --run [--split] / test     → OWN re-implementation: no baselines, slices=[per_task],
 │                                          default batch → OVERWRITES evaluation/test_overall.json
 │                                          (val → "test_overall.json") · leaves rest of run stale
 │   build-intervention ... [--apply]    → catalog episodes → block → yaml.safe_dump (comments lost)
 │                                          imbalanced → kind: custom · dup _target_episodes
 │   rollout --run                       → append "unavailable"  (honest placeholder)
 │   export --run --output               → copy figs/eval/config/manifests (no git/env/reports; tables/ empty)
 └─ READ-ONLY
     inspect · profile · validate · analyze-failures · compare (rich table positional; CSV by key)
 scripts/*.py = app(["<cmd>", *argv])  (7 of 11 commands; == python -m model_training.cli)

Drill — answer from memory, files closed

  1. Which mtrain commands change state, and what does each one change?
  2. Why didn't you just have mtrain evaluate call the runner's evaluation code?
  3. What breaks in production: a colleague runs mtrain test --run <published run> to "check it still works" the day before a paper deadline. What has happened?
  4. What does build-intervention --apply do to configs/experiments/imbalanced.yaml, exactly?
  5. Interviewer framing: "Walk me through how a researcher uses your tool from a fresh dataset to a comparison table."

Appendix A — Model answers to the drill

A1. State-changing commands. "Four. train runs the whole pipeline, so it creates a run directory, engine dataset versions, index rows and engine failure records. evaluate, and its alias test, reload the selected checkpoint and overwrite test_overall.json and stratified.json in that run's evaluation directory. build-intervention --apply rewrites the target config file's intervention block. And rollout appends an 'unavailable' record to the run's rollout log. Everything else (inspect, profile, validate, analyze-failures, compare, and export apart from writing its own output directory) only reads. The one I'd change is evaluate, because it's the only command that mutates an existing run's results."

Why that answer lands: it's a complete inventory organized by command–query separation, and it ends on the problem command.

A2. Why not reuse the runner's evaluation? "I should have. The evaluation stage is inline in ExperimentRunner.run, so there was no function to call, and evaluate re-implemented a subset of it. The result drifted. It doesn't re-run baselines even though its docstring says it does, it hard-codes the per-task slice instead of reading the run's configured slices, it uses default batch settings, and it only rewrites two of the files the runner writes, so the predictions, summary, report and feedback all describe the old numbers. The fix is to factor stage 4 out into one function that takes the run's own resolved config, call it from both places, and have re-scores write to a new versioned directory instead of overwriting."

Why that answer lands: it gives the root cause (no factored function), the concrete drift, and the fix, including the overwrite policy. That shows you know why duplication happened, not just that it did.

A3. mtrain test the day before a deadline. "They've changed the published numbers. test is an alias for evaluate --split test, which reloads the selected checkpoint and overwrites test_overall.json and stratified.json. SmolVLA samples actions stochastically, so the new MSE differs by up to a few percent from what's in the paper's tables, and there's no backup of the old file. The stratified file has also lost its length slices, because the command only computes per-task. Meanwhile summary.json, the report, the model card and the predictions file still have the original numbers, so the run directory now contradicts itself. Recovery is from git if the paper-ready export happened earlier, but the export doesn't include the predictions or the report. The fix is that re-scoring never overwrites, writing a timestamped sibling directory instead."

Why that answer lands: it gives the precise mechanism, the stochastic amplification, the internal inconsistency and the incomplete recovery path. It shows real operational thinking.

A4. --apply on imbalanced.yaml. "It loads the YAML, replaces the whole intervention block with the resolved one, a custom block listing the kept episode IDs plus the variable, value and reason, and dumps the file back with PyYAML. Two side effects. First, PyYAML doesn't preserve comments, so the twenty-line header explaining that it's a template, why the variable must come from a profile and what the result can't claim is deleted, and the folded hypothesis strings are reflowed. Second, the kind becomes custom, so the run records the intervention as custom, not imbalanced, everywhere except the tags. I'd use a round-trip YAML library, or write the resolved block to a separate file the loader merges, and record kind: imbalanced with explicit episode IDs, which the schema already allows."

Why that answer lands: it's exact about what happens and why (PyYAML semantics), identifies both the human-facing and the data-facing consequences, and offers fixes that fit the existing schema.

A5. "From a fresh dataset to a comparison table." "The dataset has to be ingested and cleaned in the data engine first, giving something like clean/v1. Then mtrain profile on that version to see what actually varies. For SO-100 that showed a single task, which is why episode length became the imbalance variable. mtrain train with the baseline config builds a training version with lineage, checks the splits, fine-tunes, evaluates against both baselines, and writes the report, model card and feedback artifact. For a targeted experiment, mtrain build-intervention in dry-run mode resolves the episodes from the real profile; you inspect them and then --apply. Train that config, then mtrain compare with --runs repeated for each run. Three cautions: excluding interventions are scored on different test splits, --seed doesn't change the training seed yet, and mtrain test overwrites results, so I'd tell a new user to treat run directories as read-only."

Why that answer lands: it's a correct end-to-end workflow, grounded in the real data story, and it volunteers the three operational traps a new user would hit.

Module 12 Summary — the one page to revise

What this module is. An eleven-command Typer CLI with lazy engine imports. train delegates to the runner, build-intervention resolves template configs from real metadata with a dry run by default, rollout records "unavailable", and the rest are read-only views. evaluate/test is a divergent re-implementation that overwrites published results.

Five claims to make cold

  1. Only typer, yaml and rich load at import, so --help and the read-only commands never load the engine or torch.
  2. build-intervention is dry-run by default and resolves without building. train creates the version and its lineage.
  3. evaluate/test overwrite test_overall.json for a stochastic model, with different slices and no baselines, and leave the run inconsistent.
  4. --split val writes validation into test_overall.json, and the API doc recommends it.
  5. --apply deletes the template's explanatory comments and relabels imbalanced as custom.

Decisions

ChoseOverBecauseCost
Lazy imports per commandtop-level importsfast startup; no torch for viewsnone
train delegates to the runnerCLI-side logicsingle implementation--seed inherits the runner bug
evaluate re-implemented inlineshared stage functionrunner stage wasn't factoreddrift; overwrite; inconsistency
Dry run by defaultapply by defaultconfig rewrites need reviewnone
PyYAML round tripruamel round-tripalready a dependencycomments and formatting lost
rollout placeholder commandomit until realabsence is recordedduplicate untimestamped records
Script wrappersrely on python -mPATH convenienceredundant, drifting docs

Volunteer unprompted: the overwrite plus stochasticity; val into test_overall.json; comment erasure; the custom relabel; export missing git and env; the compare.py usage error; no CLI tests.

Numbers and names: 11 commands (inspect, profile, train, validate, evaluate, test, rollout, compare, analyze-failures, export, build-intervention); 7 script wrappers; --dry-run/--apply default dry; --oversample-factor 3.0; inspect --version default training/v1; evaluate slices ["per_task"], batch 32; export subdirectories figures, tables (empty), metrics, configs, manifests.

Sentences worth memorizing

  • "Every state-changing command should be obvious from its name; mtrain test overwrites published numbers, and that's the first thing I'd fix."
  • "Two implementations of evaluation drifted within one codebase. Factor the stage once, call it twice."
  • "A re-score of a stochastic model should be a new artifact, never an overwrite."
  • "My config rewriter deleted the comments that told the user what not to claim."

Module 13 — The test suite, and the constraint → decision map

Files covered: tests/conftest.py, tests/unit/test_config.py, tests/unit/test_deployment.py, tests/unit/test_evaluation_offline.py, tests/unit/test_experiment_runner.py, tests/unit/test_failure_modes.py, tests/unit/test_interventions.py, tests/unit/test_profile.py, tests/unit/test_split_integrity.py, tests/integration/test_full_chain.py, tests/smoke/test_smolvla_smoke.py

Where we are: Modules 1–12 read the code adversarially. This module reads the tests the same way: what they prove, what they assume, and which of the gaps found so far a test should have caught. It then closes the code modules with the capstone, a table tracing every non-obvious design choice back to the constraint that forced it, plus the choices no constraint explains.

The one-line thesis: The suite's distinctive strength is that it tests against a real engine on a real synthetic dataset, not mocks, so the integration seams are exercised for real. But the fixture is too uniform to exercise the logic that matters (length buckets), the most important guarantees (val-only selection, seed propagation, no-overwrite of results) have no test at all, and several tests pin the presence-only behaviour that later modules showed to be wrong.

13.1 Prerequisites — the absolute basics

  • pytest fixture. A function decorated @pytest.fixture whose return value is injected into any test that names it as a parameter. Fixtures can depend on other fixtures, forming a graph. Default scope is per test: rebuilt for each test function.
  • tmp_path. A built-in fixture giving each test a fresh temporary directory.
  • pytest.importorskip("torch"). Import the module, or skip the whole test file with a reason if it's missing.
  • Mocks vs fakes vs real collaborators. A mock records calls and returns canned answers. A fake is a small working substitute (_FakeView in Module 5). A real collaborator is the production object. Tests with real collaborators catch integration bugs and are slower and coupled to the collaborator's behaviour.
  • Test pyramid. Many fast unit tests, fewer integration tests, very few end-to-end tests. In practice, a project's "unit" folder may contain integration tests in disguise.
  • Characterization test. A test that pins current behaviour, whether or not that behaviour is correct. It's useful for refactoring, and dangerous when it pins a bug as a contract.
  • Coverage vs assurance. A line being executed by a test (coverage) is different from its outcome being checked (assurance). A test that only asserts a file exists covers a lot and assures little.
  • Global state in tests. set_settings(s) mutates a process-wide singleton. Tests sharing it can't safely run in parallel (pytest-xdist).

13.2 Inventory, verified

FileTestsNeeds torch?What it exercises
unit/test_config.py8nomerge, templates, sum-to-one, identity, resolved dump
unit/test_deployment.py6nohealth_check (5), registry round-trip (1)
unit/test_evaluation_offline.py6nobaselines, error distribution, episode metrics, temporal, stratified, unknown slice
unit/test_experiment_runner.py3nobaseline-only full run, no-overwrite, reduced recorded

Total5652 run in the light venv (README line 282 says "52 unit + integration": verified)

I couldn't run any of them (Module 1: the engine isn't in this checkout). Everything below is from reading.

13.3 conftest.py: the real-engine fixture graph

"""Shared fixtures: a real synthetic dataset taken through raw -> clean ->
training, exactly the way `rde run` would, so every layer of model_training is
tested against real rde objects rather than mocks."""

@pytest.fixture
def settings(tmp_path) -> Settings:
    s = Settings(paths=Paths(tmp_path / "rde_data"), row_group_size=64, batch_size=32)
    s.paths.ensure()
    set_settings(s)
    return s

@pytest.fixture
def source_dataset(tmp_path) -> Path:
    # Two tasks (not the single-task default) so episode_id-by-task_id
    # selection -- what the imbalanced/rebalanced interventions use -- has
    # something real to select between.
    return make_lerobot_dataset(tmp_path / "source", num_episodes=8, frames_per_episode=20, fps=10,
        tasks=("Pick up the cube and place it in the box.", "Push the block to the target."))

@pytest.fixture
def raw(settings, source_dataset, catalog):
    return ingest(source_dataset, "so100_test", "lerobot", "raw/v1", settings=settings, catalog=catalog)

@pytest.fixture
def clean_version(settings, catalog, raw) -> str:
    TransformPipeline([NormalizeVector("state.joint"), NormalizeVector("action.joint")], ...).run(
        "so100_test", "raw/v1", "clean/v1")
    # `rde run` always validates before a version is considered usable
    # downstream; without this, every episode sits at quality_status=UNKNOWN
    # and DatasetBuilder.build() (which admits only PASSED/WARNING by
    # default) would refuse to build a training version from it.
    QCEngine(settings=settings, catalog=catalog).run("so100_test", "clean/v1")
    return "clean/v1"

@pytest.fixture
def training_version(settings, catalog, clean_version) -> str:
    DatasetBuilder(...).build("so100_test", clean_version, "training/v1",
                              splits={"train": 0.6, "val": 0.2, "test": 0.2}, seed=7)
    return "training/v1"

Decision: real engine objects, not mocks.

  • Why: this repository's risk is almost entirely at the boundary with the engine (Module 1's import table). A mocked DatasetBuilder would pass while the real one changed. test_reduced_intervention_selects_half_and_records_lineage can check real lineage records only because the catalog is real. That's the strongest test in the suite.
  • Alternative: mock rde, so tests run without the engine and fast.
  • Cost: (1) the suite can't run without the sibling engine checkout, so nothing runs in this checkout; (2) every test pays for ingest, normalize, QC and build per test (function scope, rebuilt each time); (3) tests are coupled to engine behaviour, and an engine change can break a test here without any change here, with no version pin to warn you (Module 1).
  • The comments are excellent. They explain why each fixture step exists: two tasks so task-based selection has something to select; QC so the builder admits episodes. Those are the non-obvious facts a new contributor needs.
  • set_settings(s) mutates global state. It's correct for sequential runs. With pytest -n auto (xdist), workers are separate processes, so it's actually safe there too, but any thread-based parallelism or test that forgets the settings fixture would pick up the previous test's settings.

The fixture's shape limits what can be tested:

  • 8 episodes × 20 frames, all the same length. Every length-bucket computation over the fixture collapses (all "short", by the <= tie rule from Module 4). The test file itself says so: "The synthetic fixture's episodes are all the same length, so every episode buckets to 'short'". Only the pure test_compute_length_bucket_splits_into_terciles exercises real terciles.
  • A 0.6/0.2/0.2 split of 8 episodes gives a test split of about 2 episodes (exact sizes depend on the engine's quantile rounding). stratified_evaluate's length slices require >= 3 test episodes (Module 7), so test_stratified_evaluate_per_task_and_length_bucket almost certainly never executes the per_length_bucket branch. Its assertions (an overall key exists, some task_ key exists, slices ⊆ overall) all pass with the length branch silently skipped. The test's name claims coverage it doesn't get.
  • Two tasks is good for task selection, but the real primary dataset has one task, so the code path SO-100 actually runs (single task, so task_0 duplicates overall, Module 7) isn't the one the fixture tests.

13.4 What the tests prove well

  1. Interventions create real lineage (test_reduced_intervention_selects_half_and_records_lineage). The transform run's kept and dropped IDs equal the computed ones. This is the claim of the project, and it's verified end to end.
  2. Weights reach the built version (test_rebalanced_intervention_keeps_all_episodes_and_sets_weights): manifest.statistics["weighted_episodes"] is truthy, so the request survives into the engine.
  3. Templates fail as documented (test_rebalanced_template_fails_loudly_until_filled_in, test_imbalanced_template_loads_but_is_empty).
  4. The no-overwrite rule (test_runner_refuses_to_overwrite_an_existing_run).
  5. Failure modes raise specific exceptions (test_failure_modes.py): unknown dataset or version raises LookupError, an unsupported variation raises ValueError with "no automatic rule", a corrupt checkpoint raises ValueError with "incomplete". The module docstring states the standard well: "every one of these must raise a specific, actionable error -- never degrade silently".
  6. The runner completes without torch (test_runner_completes_baseline_only_experiment). It uses model: mean_action to exercise every non-training stage in the light venv, and asserts "unavailable" in report_text, so the rollout honesty rule is tested.
  7. Cross-artifact consistency, once: test_split_integrity_then_profile_then_stratified_eval asserts profile.splits == report.split_sizes. That's a good pattern, and it's used only here.
  8. Cross-embodiment feature shapes (smoke): the ALOHA-shaped contract gives 28-dimensional state (14 + 14) and 14-dimensional action, with normalization stats concatenated in feature order. This guards a real bug class (Module 2's note that ALOHA "generalized after a fix").
  9. The SmolVLA chain runs end to end (smoke): 4 real optimizer steps, checkpoint reload, all artifacts present, loss not NaN (final_train_loss == final_train_loss, the classic NaN self-inequality idiom). The non-divisor batch_size: 3 was chosen deliberately to reproduce the action-queue bug ("predict() must never reuse a cached action chunk across unrelated batches"). That's a regression test written from a real incident, which is the best kind.

13.5 What the tests miss, mapped to the gaps they would have caught

Gap (module)Test that would have caught itExists?
--seed doesn't reach training.seed; replicates share experiment_id (M11-1)run twice with different seeds, assert training.seed and the experiment_ids differno
Selection never uses test (the core guarantee, M6)spy on Evaluator.evaluate during Trainer.train, assert every split == "val"no. It's enforced only by reading the code
Unknown YAML keys silently ignored (M2-1)load a config with learning_rat:, expect an errorno
model: SmolVLA silently skips training (M2-2)expect a validation errorno
Imbalanced with no value becomes a full run (M2-4)assert raise or non-empty excluded_episode_idsno
frac=0.0 fails in the builder (M4-2)call build_intervention_dataset with 0.0no. The existing test checks the helper and asserts the opposite
Filter-then-split moves the test set (M4-1)assert reduced-run test IDs ⊆ baseline test IDsno
Overlap check can't fail on real views (M5-1)— (the fake view test is all that's possible)n/a
Unassigned or empty splits pass (M5-2)build with val: 0.0, expect integrity failureno
Coverage normalization (M5-4)two-bin bimodal counts → expect < 1.0no (only asserts 0 ≤ x ≤ 1)
_prune_checkpoints (M6-10)pure tmp_path testno
crash.json on exception; signals (M6-2)make fit raise, assert crash.jsonno
NaN locks best (M6-4)feed a NaN val metricno
Slices reconcile with overall (M7-1)frame-weighted slice MSE ≈ overallno (stochastic models only; baselines are deterministic, so it would pass for the wrong reason)
Feedback branches reachable (M8-2/3)synthetic outcomes concentrated in one episodeno. The integration test checks shape
health_check cross-check (M9-1)mismatched action dimension, expect failureno. The existing test passes a mismatched, empty checkpoint
DeployedModel.load/predict (M9-4)any callno
Report content (git n/a, MAE n/a; M10)assert the commit appears in the reportno (only substring checks for name and "unavailable")
mtrain compare alignment, evaluate overwrite, --apply comments (M12)CLI tests via typer.testing.CliRunnerno CLI tests at all
run_data_fraction_sweep (M11-10–13)any callno
Light venv stays light (M1-7)import the runner, assert "torch" not in sys.modulesno
Registry "most recent wins" (M9-11)register two rows, get returns the secondno

Pattern: the suite tests that each component works on the happy path and fails loudly on bad identity (unknown versions, missing files). It doesn't test cross-stage invariants (seed flows through, test never selects, slices partition overall, artifacts agree with each other), and that's where every serious bug in this manual lives.

Tests that pin wrong behaviour (characterization hazards)

  • test_reduced_intervention_at_zero_fraction_is_honored asserts the helper returns [] for 0.0, encoding a contract the system can't honour. Its docstring argues 0.0 is a "legitimate, explicit request".
  • test_health_check_passes_on_well_formed_checkpoint_and_contract passes an empty model.safetensors and metadata naming dataset_id: "d" against an SO-100 contract. Fixing Module 9's cross-check would turn this test red, correctly.
  • test_invalid_deployment_contract_rejected (failure modes) uses rde_checkpoint_meta.json = "{}", so the same presence-only stance.
  • test_experiment_id_is_deterministic_given_same_inputs is true only with a pinned created_at, which no YAML path provides. Its name overstates the property.

Smoke-test realities

  • It needs network on first run (it downloads lerobot/smolvla_base from the Hugging Face Hub) and a torch plus lerobot install. It's skipped otherwise, "never replaced with a stub" (docstring). That's honest. It also means no CI could run it without Hub access and a GPU or a patient CPU.
  • The three feature-builder tests are skipped in the light venv because importorskip("torch") is at module top, even though they test pure contract → shape logic. If those builders return lerobot PolicyFeature objects (likely, given .shape), lerobot is genuinely required. It's unverifiable here.
  • It imports two private engine functions (_policy_features_from_contract, _dataset_stats_from_contract), which is Module 1's private-API coupling.
  • The end-to-end smoke asserts existence, not values. "mse" in test_overall["metrics"] and files exist. With 4 steps that's appropriate (no learning is expected), but a sanity bound (for example, SmolVLA MSE finite and positive) would catch a NaN in evaluation, not just in training.

13.6 The capstone: constraint → decision map

Each row traces a non-obvious choice to the named constraint that forced it, with the evidence for the constraint. The second table lists the choices no constraint explains. Those are the ones to call arbitrary, rather than invent a justification for.

Constraints that should have forced a decision, but didn't

C2 + C10 offline vs realkeep feedback decisions off the test splitthe curation loop runs on test (M8)

That last table is the most important thing you can say about this project's design: the constraints were correctly identified, and most were honoured in the mechanism layer (data versions, honesty markers, memory). The statistical consequences of C3 and C6, though, were not carried through into evaluation and reporting.

13.7 Honest gaps to own before an interviewer finds them

  1. There's no test for any cross-stage invariant: val-only selection, seed propagation, slices partitioning overall, artifacts agreeing with each other. Consequence: the bugs in Modules 6, 7, 8 and 11 were all invisible to the suite.
  2. The fixture's uniform episode lengths and a 2-episode test split mean length-bucket logic is never exercised on real data, and test_stratified_evaluate_per_task_and_length_bucket almost certainly skips its length branch. Consequence: the test's name claims coverage it doesn't get.
  3. Some tests pin wrong behaviour (zero fraction "honoured", presence-only health checks with empty weights, "deterministic" IDs). Consequence: fixing the bugs will break these tests, which is correct, but they currently read as specifications.
  4. There are no tests for the CLI, the reports, the sweep, _prune_checkpoints, crash.json, DeployedModel.load/predict or registry ordering. Consequence: large surfaces are unexercised.
  5. The suite can't run without the sibling engine, and the smoke test needs Hub network plus torch. Consequence: no CI is possible as configured, and "52 pass" is a local, dated claim.
  6. Function-scoped fixtures rebuild ingest, QC and build per test. Consequence: a slow suite (unmeasured here); scope="session" for read-only fixtures would help.
  7. Global set_settings in a fixture. Consequence: hidden coupling between tests that forget the fixture.
  8. The smoke test asserts existence, not value sanity, in evaluation. Consequence: a NaN MSE in evaluation would pass.
  9. The private-engine-API import in the smoke test. Consequence: brittle against legitimate engine refactors.

The shape to carry into the cross-cutting modules

 tests/conftest.py   make_lerobot_dataset(8 eps × 20 frames, 2 tasks, uniform length)
   settings(tmp, set_settings) → raw/v1 (ingest) → clean/v1 (normalize + QC) → training/v1 (0.6/0.2/0.2, seed 7)
        │ real engine objects, no mocks
 ┌──────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 unit (49): config · interventions+lineage ✓ · split (fake leak) · profile · offline eval
            runner (baseline-only ✓, no-overwrite ✓) · deployment (presence-only ✓✗) · failure modes ✓
 integration (3): view→adapter→model→Prediction→Evaluator→feedback (shape)
 smoke (4, torch+Hub): feature shapes (cross-embodiment ✓) · 4-step SmolVLA run (existence)
 └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   MISSING: cross-stage invariants · CLI · reports · sweep · prune · crash · DeployedModel.predict

 Constraints → decisions:  C1 GPU · C2 no robot · C3 tiny data · C4 schema · C5 engine · C6 stochastic
                           C7 deps · C8 embodiment · C9 team · C10 spec rules · C11 unreliable runtime
   honoured in mechanisms ✓        statistical consequences of C3/C6 not carried through ✗

Drill — answer from memory, files closed

  1. Why does the suite use a real engine instead of mocks, and what does that cost?
  2. Name three bugs from this manual that a test would have caught, and the test you'd write for each.
  3. Why didn't you just write more unit tests with mocks so the suite runs without the engine?
  4. What breaks in production: you set up CI on a standard GitHub runner tomorrow. What happens?
  5. Interviewer framing: "Which of your design decisions were forced by constraints, and which were just choices?"

Appendix A — Model answers to the drill

A1. Real engine vs mocks. "This package is mostly glue around the data engine. It decides which episodes to keep, asks the engine to build versions, reads them back, and scores through the engine's evaluator. So the risk sits almost entirely at that boundary, and mocks would hide exactly the bugs I care about. With a real engine on a synthetic dataset, I can assert that the engine's own lineage record shows the same kept and dropped episodes my code computed, which is the project's core claim. The costs: the suite can't run without the sibling engine checkout, each test rebuilds ingest, QC and build, so it's slower, and an engine change can break my tests with no warning because the engine isn't version-pinned. I'd keep the real-engine tests, scope read-only fixtures to the session for speed, and pin an engine version range."

Why that answer lands: it justifies the choice by where the risk is, cites the specific test it enables, and owns all three costs with mitigations.

A2. Three bugs a test would have caught. "First, the seed bug: run the same config with seed=0 and seed=1 and assert the resolved training seed differs. Today it doesn't, so replicates are fake. Second, the zero-fraction sweep point: call build_intervention_dataset with fraction 0.0. The existing test only calls the helper and asserts it returns an empty list, which passes while the real path raises. Third, the health check: give it an ALOHA-shaped checkpoint and an SO-100 contract and expect DeploymentHealthError. Today the test suite actually passes an empty weights file with mismatched metadata. The pattern is that my tests check each component in isolation and miss invariants that span stages, so I'd add a small set of cross-stage invariant tests: seed flows through, selection only ever calls the evaluator on val, slices partition the overall predictions."

Why that answer lands: it gives three concrete tests tied to real bugs, explains why the existing tests missed them, and generalizes to a testing strategy.

A3. Why not mock everything? "Because the bugs that matter here live in how my code and the engine agree, and a mock encodes my assumption about the engine, so it agrees with my code by construction. For example, a mocked builder would never reveal that splits are assigned after filtering, which is the cause of the moving test set. Where mocks would help is the pure logic: pruning, threshold arithmetic, report rendering, CLI argument handling. None of that needs the engine, and none of it is tested today. So the answer is both: real-engine integration tests for the boundary, and fast, engine-free unit tests for the pure functions I currently don't test at all."

Why that answer lands: it explains the specific epistemic weakness of mocks with an example from your own system, then identifies where they would add value. A nuanced answer, not a dogmatic one.

A4. CI on a GitHub runner tomorrow. "Installation fails first. The engine dependency is a bare name resolved only through a uv-specific sibling-path source, so the runner would need to check out both repositories side by side and install with uv. If you fix that, the 52 light tests should run, though I've never measured how long, since fixtures rebuild a dataset per test. The smoke test would skip, because there's no torch. If you installed torch, it would try to download the SmolVLA base checkpoint from the Hugging Face Hub and run on CPU, so it would be slow and network-dependent. I'd run the light suite on every push and the smoke test nightly on a GPU runner with a cached Hub download, and add one job that imports the runner and asserts torch wasn't loaded."

Why that answer lands: it predicts the failure sequence in order, separates the fast and slow tiers, and proposes a realistic CI layout.

A5. "Which decisions were forced by constraints, and which were just choices?" "Forced: the small batch, the 32-frame validation cap and freeing GPU memory before evaluation all come from an 8-gigabyte laptop GPU. Offline-only evaluation and the 'unavailable' rollout come from having no robot or simulator. Exact-size episode splits and the separate split seed come from having 48 episodes. Using episode length as the imbalance variable comes from SO-100 being single-task with no variation field. Interventions as versioned transforms come from the data engine owning immutable versions. Just choices: the 90th-percentile failure threshold, the 0.5 concentration cut, 3× oversampling, keeping three checkpoints, and seed 42. Those are arbitrary, and in the threshold case they're inconsistent. And the interesting part is a third list, constraints that should have forced decisions but didn't. A tiny test set and a stochastic policy should have forced bootstrap intervals, real multi-seed runs, a frozen test split across interventions and predict-once slicing. I honoured the constraints in the mechanisms and didn't carry them through to the statistics."

Why that answer lands: it gives a clean three-way classification with evidence, labels arbitrary choices as arbitrary, and ends with a sharp self-critique that shows design maturity. It's the single answer that best summarizes the project.

Module 13 Summary — the one page to revise

What this module is. A 56-test suite (52 torch-free) built on a real engine and a real synthetic dataset, strong at boundary integration and loud failure modes, absent on cross-stage invariants. It's followed by the capstone map tracing each design choice to one of twelve constraints, and naming the arbitrary choices and the constraints whose statistical consequences weren't carried through.

Five claims to make cold

  1. Real engine, no mocks: the lineage test proves interventions are real engine transforms.
  2. 49 unit + 3 integration = 52 light tests (README verified), plus 4 smoke tests needing torch and Hub network.
  3. The fixture's uniform lengths and 2-episode test split mean length-slice logic is effectively untested.
  4. No test checks val-only selection, seed propagation, slice reconciliation, CLI, reports, sweep or pruning.
  5. Constraints were honoured in mechanisms (memory, lineage, honesty) but not in statistics (C3 tiny data, C6 stochastic policy).

Decisions

ChoseOverBecauseCost
Real engine fixturesmocksrisk is at the engine boundaryno engine means no tests; slow; unpinned coupling
Synthetic 8 × 20, 2 tasksreal data samplefast, deterministic, task selection possibleuniform lengths; tiny test split
importorskip smokestubbed SmolVLAnever fake the modelneeds torch plus Hub; private-API import
Fake view for leak detectionnoneonly way to reach the failure branchproves the check is near-tautological
Baseline-model runner testskip runner in light venvfull pipeline without torchtraining path untested in the light venv
Function-scoped fixturessession scopeisolationrebuild cost per test

Volunteer unprompted: no cross-stage invariant tests; the length-branch test that doesn't exercise its branch; tests pinning bugs; no CLI or report tests; CI impossible as configured; the capstone's "should have forced" table.

Numbers and names: 56 tests (8 / 6 / 6 / 3 / 10 / 9 / 3 / 4 unit, 3 integration, 4 smoke); fixture so100_test, 8 episodes × 20 frames at 10 fps, 2 tasks, splits 0.6/0.2/0.2 with seed 7; smoke: 4 steps, batch 2, ckpt and val every 2, keep 1, eval batch 3 (non-divisor, deliberate), max_frames 20; constraints C1–C12; RTX 4060 8 GB, about 10 GB RAM, lerobot 0.4.4, torch 2.10.

Sentences worth memorizing

  • "I test against the real engine because a mock would agree with my assumptions by construction."
  • "My tests check components; my bugs live between them."
  • "Every non-obvious decision traces to a constraint, except the thresholds, which are arbitrary, and I say so."
  • "I honoured the constraints in the mechanisms and failed to carry them through to the statistics."

Module 14 — Low-Level Design

Files covered: all of src/model_training/ (cross-cutting). No new files; every claim here points back to a code module.

Where we are: Modules 1–13 walked the code one stage at a time. This module looks at the same code as a design: how it's layered, which patterns it actually uses (with exact locations), where SOLID holds or strains, which boundaries are typed and which are bare dicts, how a run moves through its states, how errors travel, and what's safe to share or repeat. It's the material for the "draw me the class and sequence diagrams" part of an interview.

The one-line thesis: The package has a clean, acyclic, strictly downward dependency graph with a single composition root. But almost every boundary between stages is an untyped dict[str, Any], the only real state machine is implicit in which files exist, and the error model is "raise built-in exceptions and let them escape". So the structure is sound, and the contracts are weak.

14.1 Prerequisites — the absolute basics

  • Layering / dependency rule. Code is arranged in layers, and each layer may import only layers below it. Cycles are forbidden. That's what lets you change a high layer without touching low ones.
  • Composition root. The one place where concrete collaborators are created and wired together. Everything else receives its collaborators.
  • Design pattern. A named, reusable solution shape (Gang of Four, Fowler's Patterns of Enterprise Application Architecture). Naming one is useful only if you can point to where it is and say what it buys.
  • SOLID.
    • Single responsibility: one reason to change.
    • Open/closed: extend without editing.
    • Liskov substitution: subtypes are usable wherever their base type is.
    • Interface segregation: no client depends on methods it doesn't use.
    • Dependency inversion: depend on abstractions, not concretions.
  • Typed contract. A boundary whose data shape is declared and checked (a Pydantic model, a dataclass with type hints checked by mypy, a TypedDict). dict[str, Any] is an untyped contract: the shape lives only in the code that reads it.
  • Sequence diagram. The order of calls between components for one scenario.
  • State machine. The set of states an entity can be in and the legal transitions between them.
  • Idempotent operation. Doing it twice has the same effect as doing it once.
  • Thread safety. Correct behaviour when called from several threads at once. Process safety is the same across processes, which matters for files and catalogs.

14.2 Layering and the dependency rule

Every internal import, gathered with grep:

                         ┌─────────┐
            Layer 4      │  cli    │  (lazy imports; composition for the human)
                         └────┬────┘
                         ┌────▼─────────────┐
            Layer 3      │  experiments     │  runner = COMPOSITION ROOT; data_fraction → runner
                         └─┬──┬──┬──┬──┬──┬─┘
          ┌────────────────┘  │  │  │  │  └──────────────────┐
     ┌────▼────┐   ┌──────────▼┐ │ ┌▼──────────┐   ┌────────▼───┐
L2   │training │   │   data    │ │ │evaluation │   │ deployment │   feedback   reports
     │→config, │   │ →config   │ │ │ (rde only)│   │ →tracking  │   (rde only) (no internal
     │ tracking│   └───────────┘ │ └───────────┘   │   .index   │               imports)
     └────┬────┘                 │                 └─────┬──────┘
     ┌────▼─────────────┐        │                       │
L1   │ config  tracking │◄───────┴───────────────────────┘
     └──────────────────┘   (config: pydantic+yaml only; tracking: stdlib only)
                 all layers ─────────────▶ rde (external engine)

Verified properties:

  • There are no cycles. No module imports experiments or cli except the CLI itself.
  • The leaves are leaves. config imports only pydantic and yaml; tracking imports only the standard library (plus optional torch, lerobot and psutil for observation).
  • reports imports nothing internal. It takes plain dicts. That's maximal decoupling, which is also why its inputs are untyped (14.5).
  • evaluation and feedback import only rde. They're pure adapters over engine services.
  • deployment.registry depends on tracking.index, the one lateral, cross-feature reuse, and it's a reasonable one (shared JSONL mechanism).

Where the rule bends:

  • The CLI reaches below the façade. It imports model_training.config.schema.DatasetIntervention and model_training.data.interventions.resolve_episode_subset / compute_length_bucket directly, and reimplements both _target_episodes and evaluation (Module 12). The CLI depends on internals instead of a service API.
  • Everything depends directly on rde, a concrete external package, with no abstraction layer of our own. rde is effectively layer 0 for every layer. That's a deliberate choice (Module 1: the model layer "never owns the data"), with the cost that there's no seam for substituting the engine. Tests use the real engine (Module 13) partly because there's nothing to mock against.
  • Import-time side effects cross layers. reports.figures sets the matplotlib backend globally (Module 10), and deployment.artifact imports the engine's model module eagerly (Module 1). Layers are separated by imports, but not by effects.

Composition root: ExperimentRunner.__init__ / run builds Catalog, DataEngine, ExperimentIndex, Trainer, Evaluator (via helpers), SmolVLAPolicy and VisionLanguageAdapter. The CLI builds ExperimentRunner() with defaults. That's a good shape: one place to change wiring.

Patterns named in docs or comments that aren't really there: a "registry" in the MLflow sense (no promotion, Module 9), a "flywheel" as a decision loop (Module 8), and a "matrix" (build_model_dataset_matrix, Module 10).

14.4 SOLID, and where each principle is strained

PrincipleWhere it holdsWhere it's strained
Ssplit_integrity.py (one check), JsonlIndex, _deep_merge, figures._saveExperimentRunner.run (orchestration plus JSON formatting plus artifact naming plus limitation text, about 250 lines); cli.evaluate (re-scoring plus persistence plus formatting); DatasetProfile builder (stats, coverage, QC, splits, notes)
Onew baselines need only an engine MODEL_REGISTRY entry; new figures are new functionsnew intervention kinds need edits in three places (schema Literal plus validator, resolve_episode_subset, build_intervention_dataset branch) plus the CLI; new slice types need an elif in stratified_evaluate; a new model family needs the runner's if model == "smolvla" changed
Lbaselines and SmolVLA are substitutable behind the engine's Model / ModelInputAdapter for evaluation (the same Evaluator.evaluate call)Trainer only accepts SmolVLA (it raises otherwise), so "model" isn't substitutable for training. train_view and val_view are typed as DatasetView but used as the same object
Ireports take only the dict fields they renderModelArtifact carries 16 fields, and the registry and model card each use a subset. RunDir exposes write_json / append_jsonl / sub, but half the writers bypass it (Module 3)
Drunner collaborators injectable; settings and catalog passed down rather than fetched where possibleevery module depends on concrete rde classes (Catalog, DataEngine, SmolVLAPolicy, Evaluator); there's no project-owned protocol, and SmolVLAPolicy is constructed directly inside Trainer and the runner, so it can't be injected

The honest summary: SRP and DIP are the strained ones, and both for the same reason. The runner is the only seam, and it does too much, while the engine is depended on concretely everywhere.

14.5 Contract map: every boundary, and whether it's enforced

BoundaryTypeEnforced?Notes
YAML → ExperimentConfigPydantic modelspartlytypes and cross-field rules yes; unknown keys silently ignored (no extra="forbid"); free strings for model, selection_metric, slices, baseline_models (Module 2)
CLI options → functionsTyper annotationsyes, for typesemantic validity (does the version exist?) is checked only later
intervention → runnerInterventionResult dataclasstype hints only (no runtime check)its build_summary: dict[str, Any] is the engine's untyped blob
runner → dataset.jsondict literalnoshape exists only in the runner and its readers (CLI evaluate, comparison)
split check → runnerSplitIntegrityReporthints onlyok semantics too weak (Module 5)
profile → report and figuresDatasetProfile.to_dict() → dict[str, Any]noconsumers index profile["episode_length"]["histogram"][i]["lo"] etc.
engine → profileexplorer dicts (durations["duration_s"]["max"], dims[k]["per_dimension"])noundocumented engine shapes; a change means KeyError (Module 5)
engine fit → on_step(record)dict with step, loss, lr, grad_normnorecord["step"] and r["loss"] are hard key accesses (Modules 6, 10)
trainer → runnerTrainingOutcome dataclasshints onlycheckpoints: list[dict] is untyped
checkpoint directory → everything5 files plus rde_checkpoint_meta.jsonpresence only (verify_checkpoint)no content or integrity check; metadata keys untyped
EvaluationResult → offline helpersengine object (.metrics dict, .outcomes list of FrameOutcome)engine-sidemetrics keys untyped; FrameOutcome.error semantics (per-frame MAE) in docs only
offline helpers → JSONdictnoper-episode "mse" misnamed (Module 7)
feedback → artifactdictnoreport.summary() and artifacts are engine blobs
artifact → index / registryModelArtifact.to_dict()no on readModelRegistry.get returns dict
runner → reportctx: dict[str, Any] with about 15 keysnothe documented shape is "see ExperimentRunner" (docstring)
run directory → compare / CLIJSON filesno schema, no versionmissing files mean silent Nones; partial files mean JSONDecodeError
checkpoint ↔ contract (deployment)—nothe cross-check is missing (Module 9)

Pattern: the edges into the system (YAML, CLI) are typed. The interior stage-to-stage handoffs are value objects that immediately degrade to dicts, and everything persisted is schemaless JSON. A Pydantic model per persisted artifact (DatasetRecord, ModelRecord, SummaryRecord) with a schema_version field would type both the write and the read path, and it would have caught the vla_finetune / model_training key bug (Module 3) at once.

This one path exercises four separate gaps: late validation (Module 11), irreversible catalog writes before the risky step (Module 4), no status marker (Module 3) and the retry block (Module 3).

14.7 State machine of a run (implicit, reconstructed from which files exist)

There's no explicit status field. The states below are what you can infer from the run directory.

               make_run_dir
   (none) ───────────────────▶ CREATED  {environment.json, git.json, config.resolved.yaml}
                                  │ build_intervention_dataset ok
                                  ▼
                              DATA_READY {dataset.json} + index.datasets row + engine versions
                                  │ split check + profile ok
                                  ▼
                              PROFILED {split_integrity.json, dataset_profile.json}
                                  │ fit starts
                                  ▼
                              TRAINING {metrics/train.jsonl growing, checkpoints/step_N}
                       ┌──────────┼──────────────────────────────┐
     Exception in fit  │          │ fit returns                   │ SIGTERM / SIGKILL / Ctrl-C
                       ▼          ▼                               ▼
              CRASHED_TRAINING  TRAINED {checkpoints/final,     KILLED (indistinguishable from
              {crash.json}       training_outcome.json, model.json}  TRAINING: no marker)
                                  │ evaluation + feedback
                                  ▼
                              EVALUATED {evaluation/*.json, predictions/, model_artifact.json,
                                         failures/feedback_artifact.json}
                                  │ reports
                                  ▼
                              COMPLETED {reports/*.md, summary.json} + index.experiments row
                                  │ mtrain evaluate/test
                                  ▼
                              RESCORED  (test_overall.json replaced; rest stale — Module 12)

   Any exception outside fit (intervention, profile, baselines, feedback, plotting) ⇒ stuck in the
   last state reached, with no marker. Every non-(none) state blocks a same-day re-run.

Missing transitions: CRASHED/KILLED → RETRY (only possible by renaming or deleting), and RESCORED → consistent (no recompute of dependents). The fix: write status.json = {"state": ..., "updated_at": ...} atomically at each arrow, and let make_run_dir accept --force only from a terminal failure state.

14.8 Error taxonomy

ErrorRaised whereCaught whereWhat the user sees
FileNotFoundError("no config file at …")config/loader.py load_yamlnowhereCLI traceback
ValueError("must contain a YAML mapping")loader.pynowheretraceback
Pydantic ValidationError (wrapping validator ValueErrors: sums, imbalanced or rebalanced target, custom IDs, fraction)config/schema.pynowheretraceback with field paths, the clearest errors in the system
FileExistsError("run directory already exists…")tracking/run_dir.py make_run_dirnowheretraceback; message explains the fix
LookupError("unknown version")data/interventions.py, engine get_dataset, explorernowheretraceback
ValueError (no episodes / zero resolved / empty weights / no automatic rule / no episodes to upweight)data/interventions.py (6 sites)nowheretraceback, after run directory creation
SplitLeakageErrordata/split_integrity.pynowheretraceback (practically unreachable, Module 5)
profile split lookup Exceptionengineprofile.py (broad except)swallowed into a note (sometimes a misleading one)
histogram SQL Exceptionengine DuckDBprofile.pyswallowed into {"reason": ...}
git subprocess Exceptionrun_dir._git_infosameswallowed into {"error": ...} in git.json
ImportError torch / lerobot / psutilrun_dir, trainersameconverted to None plus a note (by design)
ValueError (trainer model guard, empty train IDs)training/trainer.pynowheretraceback
FileNotFoundError / ValueError from verify_checkpointenginetrainer.on_stepconverted to verified: false plus checkpoint_errors.jsonl
any Exception in fit (CUDA OOM, NaN if raised, on_step errors)engine / trainertrainer.traincrash.json written, then re-raised to a traceback
KeyboardInterrupt, SIGTERM, SIGKILLOS / usernothingprocess ends; no marker
KeyError("unknown baseline model")evaluation/offline.pynowheretraceback after training
ValueError("unknown evaluation slice")evaluation/offline.pynowheretraceback after training
KeyError on engine dict shapes (r["loss"], durations[...])profile, figures, clinowheretraceback, possibly after training
DeploymentHealthErrordeployment/loader.pynowhere (only tests call it)—
RuntimeError("call load() before predict()")deployment/loader.pynowhere—
JSONDecodeError on torn or partial filesJsonlIndex.read_all, load_run_summary, CLI readersnowheretraceback with no hint of cause (Module 3)
typer.Exit(1) with a yellow or red messagecli.py (6 sites)Typerfriendly one-line message

Shape of the error model: only two custom exception types (SplitLeakageError, DeploymentHealthError). Everything else is a built-in, nothing is caught at the top level, and the CLI turns some missing-file conditions into friendly exits but lets every pipeline error surface as a raw traceback. Swallowing is confined to observation code (git, environment, profile extras), and that's the right place for it. The conversion of errors into data (verified: false, "unavailable", crash.json) is the system's best error-handling idea, and it's applied inconsistently: there's no equivalent for failures outside fit.

14.9 Lifetime, sharing and thread safety

ObjectCreated byLifetimeShared acrossThread / process safety
Settingsget_settings() / test fixture set_settingsprocess (global singleton)everythingglobal mutable state; tests rely on fixture order
Catalogrunner __init__ (one per runner)runnerinterventions, profile, feedbackengine-side, unverified; process-level concurrency on the catalog file is unknown (version allocation race, Module 4)
DataEngine / DatasetViewrunner run, profile, CLIone run; closed in finallytrainer, evaluation, feedback (same view)single-threaded use; two views of one version open at once (runner plus profile)
ExperimentIndex / JsonlIndexrunner __init__runnerall runs in the processappends are small writes; not safe for large rows across processes, no locking (Module 3)
RunDirmake_run_dirone runrunner, trainersingle writer by convention; TOCTOU on creation
SmolVLAPolicy (train)Trainer.traintrain() call; explicitly freedclosure on_stepGPU-resident; freed before return (and not fully freed on the crash path)
SmolVLAPolicy (eval)runner runrest of runevaluation, feedbacknot explicitly freed
Evaluatorper helper callcall—stateless (inferred)
console (rich)cli.py module levelprocessall commandsfine
matplotlib backendfigures.py importprocessevery importerglobal side effect
RDE_REPO / MT_REPOrunner.py importprocessrunnerfrozen at import

No threads are created anywhere in the package. Concurrency risk is entirely multi-process: two mtrain invocations sharing runs/, experiments/*.jsonl and the engine catalog.

14.10 Idempotency table

OperationIdempotent?Second invocation does…
load_experiment_confignonew created_at, so a new experiment_id
mtrain train (same day)no, guardedFileExistsError
mtrain train (next day)nonew run directory plus new engine versions plus new index rows
build_intervention_datasetnomints new clean/vK and training/vM even for identical input
verify_split_integrity, build_dataset_profileyes (apart from generated_at)same report
Trainer.trainno (by construction)new checkpoints; stochastic
evaluate_baselinesyes, for deterministic baselinessame metrics
stratified_evaluate (SmolVLA)nostochastic re-inference, numbers move up to 5%
build_feedback_artifactnoduplicate engine failure records (persist=True)
JsonlIndex.append / record_*noduplicate rows
ModelRegistry.getyesthe latest row
mtrain evaluate / testnooverwrites with new stochastic numbers
mtrain rolloutnoappends another identical record
mtrain build-intervention --applyyes for the block, no for the filesame block; comments already gone after the first run
mtrain exportyes (overwrite)same copy; stale extra files remain
mtrain compareyessame table

Takeaway: the read paths are idempotent and the write paths are not, and nothing dedupes (versions, index rows, failure records). Content-addressing builds (Module 4) and a run status (14.7) are the two changes that would make re-execution safe.

14.11 Patterns deliberately absent, and the trigger to add each

Absent patternWhy absent is fine todayTrigger to add it
